## Document 034: 20261027_投資週報_AI資金壓力擾動市場，科技基本面仍穩健

Source PDF: all report pdf/20261027_投資週報_AI資金壓力擾動市場，科技基本面仍穩健.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資週報)
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 27 日
上週重點訊息
◼ AI 投資回報疑慮升溫，資金轉向債市避險：市場對大型科技企業 AI 基礎建設投資規模、融資
需求及未來獲利回收能力的疑慮升溫，導致科技股賣壓擴大，10 月 22 日 Nasdaq 指數下跌
約 2.6%。 隨著市場風險偏好下降 ， 避險資金轉向美國公債 ，10 年期公債殖利率單日下降約 19
個基點，創 2023 年 3 月以來最大單日跌幅，並於 23 日回升至 4.99%。市場關注焦點逐漸由
AI 投資規模轉向資本支出效益，後續大型科技企業財報及現金流表現，將成為評估 AI 產業評

價合理性的重要依據。
◼ 中東衝突推升通膨風險：中東衝突持續升溫，伊朗支持的葉門胡塞武裝攻擊沙烏地阿拉伯，引
發市場對原油供應中斷的擔憂，推升布蘭特原油價格於10 月 23 日升至每桶 116.72 美元，單
週上漲約 6.2%，創 2022 年以來最高週收盤水準。能源價格攀升加劇全球通膨壓力，並壓抑
市場風險偏好。
◼ 法國政治動盪加劇財政疑慮，歐洲主權債風險重新受到關注：法國因生活成本上升爆發大規模
抗議，進一步加深市場對政府財政整頓能力的疑慮。10 月 19 日法國 10 年期公債殖利率上升
約 10 個基點至 4.88%附近，與德國公債利差擴大至約 144 個基點，創 2012 年以來最高收盤
水準 ， 法股單日下跌約1.5%， 歐元同步走弱 。 市場擔憂政治不確定性可能削弱財政改革進度 ，
並透過主權債評價影響銀行業資產負債表。後續須關注法國財政政策、歐洲主權債利差及銀行
融資成本變化。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線宜審慎，中期聚焦基本面穩健的 AI 企業：近期 AI 資本支出、融資需求及評價調整

使科技股波動加劇 ， 但先進製程與半導體設備需求仍具韌性 ，AI 中長期成長趨勢未改變 。 隨市
場焦點由成長題材轉向投資效益，企業營收與訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率的重要性提
升 ， 具技術優勢 、 財務穩健及獲利能見度的企業相對有利 ， 而高度依賴外部融資者宜審慎看待 。
◼ 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：市場風險偏好降溫時，美國公債仍具一定
避險效果。儘管 Fed 貨幣政策路徑與能源價格增添利率不確定性，短天期債券易受政策利率
牽動，超長天期債券面臨財政赤字、國債供給及期限溢價等壓力，相較之下，中天期美債的風
險報酬較為均衡。信用債方面，投資等級企業基本面及現金流仍具韌性，後續仍可持續關注。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 聯準會上周三決議升息一碼至 3.75%-4.00%區間，FOMC 政策聲明顯示，18 名
FOMC 成員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次 ， 另有4 人認為存在升息兩次的可
能性 ，加上聯準會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)會後表達控制通膨的偏鷹立場 ， 帶動美
元指數走升至 100 高位。
⚫ 歐元區 9 月 ZEW 經濟景氣指數自前月的 31.4 降至 25.8，低於預估的 39.9。數
據下滑顯示在美伊衝突未獲緩解 、 能源價格持續高位情況下 ， 市場對於歐元區經
濟前景抱持較不樂觀的態度。
⚫ 美國公布初請失業金人數為 19.6 萬人，優於市場預期的 20.8 萬，再度呼應聯準
會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)認為目前勞動力市場仍具韌性的說法， 在美國經濟穩
健及通膨頑固的情況下，預計聯準會將持續升息，有利美元持穩。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委暨葡萄牙央行總裁佩雷拉(Alvaro Santos Pereira)表示，歐元區目前

通膨雖未廣泛擴散 ， 惟考量近期天然氣價格飆升 ， 央行將密切關注未來通膨壓力
擴散情形。最新利率期貨市場顯示，歐洲央行今年底仍有再升息一碼空間。
⚫ 日本政府通過消費稅減稅及家庭現金補貼的政策大綱，預計自明年 4 月起的兩
年內，將食品消費稅率自 8%降至 1%，惟大綱中未明確說明資金來源，再度引
發市場對於日本政府的財政擔憂，短線不利日圓表現。
⚫ 台灣央行 9/17 宣布重貼現率連 10 凍維持在 2%不變，央行總裁楊金龍表示，台
灣的通膨相較其他主要經濟體溫和，採取漸進緊縮貨幣政策有助物價穩定。 此
外，目前通膨率為 2%，明年有望降至 2%以下，後續將觀察通膨發展、主要經
濟體貨幣政策緊縮程度及台灣金融情勢，以決定未來貨幣政策走向。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.650，低點在 31.958，以 31.808 作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶 ， 美國CPI 數據公布後加強市場對於聯準會 9 月升息的預期 ，
早盤台幣跳空開低，外資連續三日賣超台股並匯出，加以出口商態度轉為惜售，令
台幣接連貶破 31.700、31.800、31.900 關卡， 週四由於聯準會偏鷹派升息，美元指
數走升至 100.300，早盤台幣跳空開低，台幣最低貶至31.958，不過隨後出口商賣壓
出籠，外資雙向進出，台幣收復部分跌幅，維持在31.900 附近震盪；臨近周末，外
資買超台股並於外匯市場匯入 ， 台幣走升至31.722， 最終在央行調節之下收在31.808。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場而言 ， 美國最新初領失業救濟金人數較預
期減少一萬多人、續領失業救濟金人數亦下降，顯示美國勞動力市場尚屬穩健，加
以聯準會升息抗通膨，鷹派立場給予美元指數支撐、不利台幣表現；就台灣市場來
看，九月台灣央行並未升息，台美利差擴大預計將帶給台幣壓力，此外，儘管聯準
會升息利空出盡，外資買超台股帶動台幣走升，惟台灣即將迎來中秋連假，預計金
融市場交投謹慎，將令台幣陷入橫盤整理格局，綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢區間
震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/21/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走升。人民幣中間價連續走升，接連升破 6.7700、6.7600 整數
關口，升至三年七個月來高點，帶動人民幣偏升，不過由於美元指數震盪走強，加
以市場靜待聯準會利率決議公布 ， 交投謹慎 ， 人民幣升幅有限 ， 大致維持高檔震盪 ，
週四聯準會鷹派升息一碼，惟人民幣中間價連續五日走升至 6.7521，並創下四年高
點，帶動人民幣緩步走升，USD/CNH 升破 6.7000 整數大關，終場境外人民幣收在
6.6944，週線升值 94 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6984，週線升值 21 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣震盪走升 。 就國際市場來看 ，FED 最新政策聲明和經濟預測顯
示，聯準會為明年繼續收緊貨幣政策敞開了大門，美元指數走升，抑制人民幣升值
的速度；就中國國內市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實
質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現； 最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中 ， 匯價維持
在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，加以USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.7000 整數關
卡，顯示 USD/CNH 空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪走升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6700-6.7200

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週日圓走貶。 週初，美國通膨數據維持高位 ，令聯準會升息預期升溫 ，USD/JPY
自 153.29 低點反彈至 155 附近；週間 ，美國聯準會宣布升息一碼 ， 帶動USD/JPY 上
漲至 156.41；臨近週末，日本央行如市場預期升息一碼，惟央行偏鴿派立場令日圓
續貶，帶動 USD/JPY 一度最高來到 158.05，而隨後在日本政府於外匯市場進行匯率
詢價下，USD/JPY 回吐部分漲幅，終場收在 156.86，週線上漲 349 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本政府上周通過消費稅減
稅及家庭現金補貼政策大綱，惟大綱中未明確說明財源，再度引發市場對於日本政
府財政惡化的擔憂，預計將不利日圓走勢。二、日本央行上周五決議將政策利率調
升一碼至 1.25%， 9 名決策委員中有 2 人對升息持反對意見，降低市場對日本央行
進一步升息的預期。另一方面 ， 聯準會上周三亦升息一碼至3.75%-4.00%區間 ，FOMC
政策聲明顯示，18 位官員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次，另有 4 人認為存在升
息兩次的可能性，聯準會升息立場明顯較日本央行更為鷹派，預計將支撐USD/JPY
走勢。三、日本財務大臣片山皋月近期再度重申，將於必要時採取外匯干預行動，
以維護外匯市場有序運行。四、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY 短中天期均
線向下發散走勢偏空，惟近期匯價在多次向下測試 154 均拉回，明顯下方有買盤支
撐。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：154.80-159.50 
前週交易區間 
153.29 158.05

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，聯準會升息預期升溫加上歐元區 9 月經濟景氣指
數下滑，令歐元走跌至 1.1525；週間，美國聯準會鷹派升息立場帶動美元走升，歐
元最低來到 1.1460；臨近週末，歐元持續偏弱整理，終場收在 1.1483，週線下跌 107
點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、 歐洲央行管委卡薩克斯
(Martins Kazaks) 表示 ， 為避免美伊戰爭引發的燃料成本上漲開始傳導至工資和其他
商品價格，歐洲央行可能需要漸進式調升利率以抑制通膨。最新利率期貨市場顯示
歐洲央行今年仍有升息一碼的空間，預計將支撐歐元走勢。二、美國聯準會決議將
利率調升一碼至 3.75%-4.00%區間，聯準會主席華許會後偏鷹言論帶動美元指數走
升至 100 上方，預計將限縮歐元走升空間。三、歐元區 9 月 ZEW 經濟景氣指數自
前月的 31.4 降至 25.8，低於預估的 39.9。數據下滑顯示在美伊衝突未獲緩解、能源
價格持續高位情況下，市場對於歐元區經濟前景抱持較不樂觀的態度，將不利歐元
表現。四、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 近期回測年線未能有效突破後明顯
拉回轉為下行走勢，目前匯價承壓於各天期均線下方，空方動能較佔優勢。綜合以
上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1350-1.1590 
前週交易區間 
1.1450 1.1599

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.14-115.09.18 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.70% 
公債－10 年期-A15106 1.92 % ~ 1.921% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.86% ~ 1.861%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 本週央行理監事會議決議結果 ， 維持政策利率不變 ， 符合
市場預期 ， 且近兩週央行持續減發定存單 ， 令市場短錢充裕 ， 惟週五銀行間分
配略為不均，部分銀行有資金需求，因此短率持穩於近期區間。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.70%，利率高位較上週上升 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投清淡，5 年期指標券 A15108 週三等殖系統成交在 1.86%，10 年
期指標券 A15106 則未成交，最近成交利率為 1.92~1.921%。 
台灣央行理監事會議如市場預期維持政策利率不變 ， 總裁楊金龍表示 ， 今年國
內通膨仍屬可控，明年有望回落至 2%以下，市場對今年升息疑慮有所降溫，
對台債形成支撐 ， 惟國際債市走勢仍偏空 ， 預期台債殖利率短線仍呈高檔區間
盤整格局，後續關注下週三財政部公布的第四季公債發行計劃，預估 5 年券
區間為 1.79%~1.83%，10 年券區間為 1.90%~1.95%。

Price target risks and methodology - UMT 
Valuation: We use a discounted P/E methodology and apply a 35.1x target P/E multiple 
to UMT’s 2029E EPS, discounting it back to 2027E at a COE of 7.8% (beta 1.2x, risk-free 
rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), which leads to our 12-month target price 
of NT$2,188. The target P/E is based on the average PEG&M ratio of peers in the 
satellite supply chain. We are Buy-rated on UMT.  
Key Risks: Slower-than-expected LEO satellite deployment; Potential competition from 
new-entrant suppliers; LEO satellite operators manufacturing in-house components.

## Document 152: 261102_citi_auras

Source PDF: all report pdf/261102_citi_auras.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Auras Technology (3324.TWO)
3Q26 Preview: Deferrals Weigh on 3Q; ASIC Ramp Underpins 
4Q26E/2027E Growth
CITI'S TAKE 
Auras is scheduled to report 3Q26E results in mid-Nov, followed by its 
annual investor meeting in the following weeks. 3Q26 revenue fell short of 
mgmt. ’s guidance of at least +15% QoQ on GB300 demand deferrals and 
Thailand flooding. We expect GPM below our prior forecast on lower scale 
and front-loaded expenses, and forecast 3Q26 net profit of NT$1.24bn, 13% 
below cons. We now model 4Q26E revenue growth of +20% QoQ on 
deferred demand and the ASIC liquid cooling ramp from Nov, and 2027E 
revenue growth of +50% YoY as multiple ASIC projects broaden the growth 
base. We raise our TP to NT$2,000 (20x 2027E EPS). Reiterate Buy.
3Q26 preview — Auras reported 3Q26 revenue of NT$9.8bn (+13% QoQ/+64% YoY), 
below mgmt. ’s earlier guidance. The shortfall mainly reflected customers deferring 
GB300 demand into 4Q26 and the flooding in Thailand. Given lower revenue scale 
and certain expenses recognized ahead of the upcoming ramp, we expect 3Q26E 
GPM to come in at 28.2% (-0.2 ppt QoQ/-1.6 ppts YoY), below our prior forecast of 
30.6%. With the opex ratio largely in line, we forecast 3Q26 net profit of NT$1.24bn 
(+28% QoQ/+27% YoY), and EPS of NT$13.32, 13% below cons.
Multiple ASIC projects to support 4Q26E/2027E — Mgmt. guided for 4Q26E 
revenue growth of +20% QoQ, driven by deferred 3Q26 revenue and the mass 
production of the ASIC project’s liquid cooling version, which we expect from Nov. 
We raise our 4Q26E growth forecast to +20% QoQ (from +16%), with GPM improving 
to 31.1%. While VR cold plate qualification is ongoing, we believe Auras is well 
positioned to win multiple ASIC projects, with a second project likely to contribute 
from 2H27. We now forecast 2027E revenue growth of +45% YoY, with GPM 
expanding to 32.2% on a rising share of customized projects and a better mix.
Implications — We lower our 2026E earnings forecast by 5% on the softer 3Q26. We 
also raise our 2027E/28E earnings forecasts by 13%/20% on higher ASIC liquid 
cooling shipment assumptions and margin expansion. Our revised TP of NT$2,000 
(from NT$1,800) remains based on 20x 2027E EPS. We believe recent concerns over 
the timing of various AI server projects are largely priced in. Given Auras’ structural 
growth in the server business, widening liquid cooling content across GPU and ASIC 
platforms, and an optimizing global manufacturing footprint, we view current 
valuation as attractive and reiterate our Buy rating.

Bull/Bear: Auras Technology (3324.TWO)

### Page 6

Auras Technology (3324.TWO)
02 November 2026 Citi Research
6
Auras Technology
Company description
Auras Technology was founded in 1998 and focused on the design and
manufacturing of thermal modules for NB/PCs. It quickly became one of
Taiwan’s top two NB cooling component suppliers. Early products included
heatsinks, fins, heat pipes, and fans for NBs, PCs, and smartphones. As
device form factor evolved, the company invested in new technologies such

as ultra-thin vapor chamber in the 2000s to address the thermal challenges
of increasingly compact consumer electronics. During the 2010s, Auras
expanded into graphic card and server cooling and was an early adopter of
liquid cooling technology. By 2012, the company had begun developing liquid
cooling component and was among the first Taiwanese firms to venture into
AI server liquid cooling.
Investment strategy
We rate Auras a Buy. Auras is emerging as a key liquid-cooling supplier as

hyperscalers expand deployment. The company has secured qualifications for
cold plates and manifolds, opening access to a wider slate of Nvidia and CSP
projects. Thailand-based production offers non-China capacity at a scale
aligned with hyperscaler sourcing plans, allowing Auras to compete more
effectively for next-generation products.
Valuation
We value Auras using a PE-based approach, reflecting its accelerating
earnings trajectory and rising share in liquid cooling market of AI servers. Our

TP of NT$2,000 is based on 20x 2027E EPS, which we believe is justified
given the company’s expanding server revenue base, deeper engagement
with CSP customers, and broader product portfolio across cold plates, CDMs,
and CDUs.
Risks
This stock is High Risk based upon our quantitative model, but assigning a
High Risk rating is not supported in our view by other qualitative factors such
as rapid RVL progress and Thailand expansion, and so a High Risk rating has

not been applied. Key downside risks to our investment thesis and target
price include: 1) slower adoption of liquid cooling; 2) heightened price
competition; 3) lower-than-expected share in liquid cooling components;
and 4) emerging alternative cooling technologies.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in

Key takeaways from Delta’s results call (October 29th) include: 
(1) Management expects both revenue and GM to go up sequentially 
in 4Q26, with some price adjustment likely to occur in 4Q26/1Q27. 
4Q GM could further improve due to some reversal in inventory 
write-offs, as the weaker-than-expected 3Q26 GM was primarily 
due to the large inventory write-offs with ~2.0ppt negative impact 
(we observed a sharp expansion in GM by 3.2ppt in 2024 following 
significant inventory write-offs of ~2.0ppt in 2023). Looking ahead, 
we continue to see increasing AI exposure for Delta to support the 
GM expansion (2026E GM at 36.1% per GSe vs. 34.3% in 2025). 
(2) The company’s BOD has approved building a new large-scale 
production site in Penang, Malaysia, and expect the site to reach 
similar scale as the Thailand operations within 5Y, subject to market 
demand (Thailand accounts for 30%+ of Delta’s total capacity per 
our estimate). 
(3) For HVDC products, the company started to see some initial 
shipment for +-400VDC solution, which is in line with expectation 
and mentioned that 800VDC solution is driven by Nvidia that is 
progressing more slowly (we expect 800VDC contribution to 
increase sharply along with the ramp up of next-generation AI 
server in 2H27). The company is positive that datacenters 
architecture will migrate increasingly toward HVDC in the longer 
term, but did mention some near term risk in the pace of 
deployment due to the shortage of skilled labor. 
(4) In terms of new products, Delta mentioned SOFC product will 
enter MP in 2027E, with the first production line having capacity of 
100MW+ (equivalent to US$300-400mn revenue contribution if fully 
utilized per GSe), which is mainly for sampling with clients. Several 
large clients have expressed interest in testing the new product, 
further capacity expansion will depend on successful verification, 
with equipment procurement requiring 6-10M (the company could

expand its SOFC capacity by multiple times within 1Y per our estimates).
(5) Delta mentioned that order visibility has extended further and noted that datacenter
deployment plans are now extended into 2030E. However, the company’s near-term
revenue and GM performance remains sensitive to the pace of client order pull-ins.
Datacenter deployment could face delays in the near term due to geopolitical risk and
shortage of components/labor/electricity, potentially leading to greater volatility in the

company’s financial performance.

(tracking faster than previous target of 25%+) and expects liquid cooling related
products to account for 13% of total revenue in 2026 (higher than previous target of
12%+).
Investment view
We maintain our positive view on Delta’s revenue/profitability outlook over the coming
years, underpinned by its increasing exposure in AI (see here). We now expect GM/OPM

will expand by 8ppt+/14ppt+ in 2028 vs. 2025, due to the increasing contribution from
the high margin AI power products (expand from 9% in 2025 to 69% in 2028E). We
believe the GM contraction in 3Q26 is manageable, with potential reversals of inventory
write-offs providing additional upside to margins going forward (historically, the
company saw solid GM expansion in 2020/2024 following significant inventory
write-offs in 2019/2023). Meanwhile, with contributions from HVDC power racks to

ramp up from 3Q26 and order visibility extending further, we expect 3Q26 GM of 34.2%
to mark the low point in 2026-28E, and we expect sequential GM expansion in every
subsequent quarter through 2028.
We believe Delta is well-positioned to capture a leading market share in the HVDC
power rack market (50-70% market share in 2027-2030E per our estimates) supported
by its industry leading AC to DC conversion capabilities. With its latest expansion in

Malaysia that is expected to reach similar scale as Thailand operations in 5Y time frame
(Thailand is currently the largest manufacturing hub for Delta), we believe Delta could
maintain its leading market share in the HVDC power rack market with its time to market
capacity. We expect HVDC solution to be the key revenue/profitability driver going
forward as its dollar content per watt to be 3x+ more than the company’s current key
PSU products.
The company continues to make progress with its new SOFC products and is expected to

ramp up in 2027 with initial capacity of 100MW+. We expect each additional 100MW of
fully utilized SOFC capacity could contribute ~1% to Delta’s 2027E revenue. We believe
Delta could capture some market share amid the electricity shortage environment, as
SOFC offers an attractive alternative power solution given its speed-to-power, reliability,
and deployment flexibility (see here).
Overall, we maintain our positive view on Delta’s long term growth prospects, supported

by its leading position in the most critical segment of the AI datacenter power industry.
We expect the company to sustain solid earnings growth over the coming year. Maintain
our Buy rating on Delta and 12m TP at NT$4,120.

We revise down our 2026E EPS by 3% by factoring the actual 3Q26 results and revise

potential shortage in materials (we revise down revenue estimates by 1/2% for 
2026/27E).

Valuation 
We reiterate our Buy rating on Delta with an unchanged 12-m TP of NT$4,120, based on 
an unchanged 26.5x P/E multiple (+1.6x STDV higher than Delta’s past 15-year average 
valuation), and maintain our valuation period at 2028E and discounted back to 2027 at a 
CoE of 11%.

Investment Thesis & PT methodology and risks

Delta Electronics is the largest vendor of power supply components globally (in terms of 
sales) and a key servo motor/inverter/PLC supplier in mainland China. It is also a key 
supplier for the EV/server power industry, holding one of the largest market shares in 
both markets. We are positive on Delta’s long-term growth opportunity driven by 
multiple factors (EV/EV charger/5G infrastructure/Datacenter power/ESS etc.) in the 
coming years. We also expect demand for its AI server power and cooling systems to 
deliver solid growth given the increasing demand for high computing performance AI 
servers, which will require higher efficiency power components and better cooling 
systems to improve the overall performance. Trading at a 1-year forward P/E multiple 
that is below our target multiple, we believe the stock is undervalued, given the 
company’s strong revenue outlook (50%+ 2025-28E CAGR) for its growth sectors 
(including EV & EV charging / green capex & renewable energy / cloud computing / 
smart manufacturing / satellite communication & smart connectivity), and its leadership

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

Update
Hon Hai Precision  | Asia Pacific
Hon Hai Technology Day 2026
(HHTD 2026)
DSX POD accelerates AI infrastructure deployment: Unveiled at GTC Berlin and
displayed at HHTD26, NVIDIA’s DSX POD packages 7x VR200 NVL72 racks, 3x
Spectrum-6 SPX racks, up to ~25 exaFLOPS of NVFP4 performance, and over 10M
components per container ( Exhibit 1 ). Hon Hai said factory integration can shorten
deployment at prepared sites from six months to as little as 14 days, with initial

deliveries expected in early 2027.
Rack capacity and automation support Rubin ramp: HH has doubled AI rack
assembly capacity over the past year to ~2K racks/week. It said its cableless Vera
Rubin compute-tray design cuts assembly time from over two hours to ~20 minutes,
with a further target of five minutes. Greater automation should improve
throughput and consistency while reducing labor intensity, according to HH
management, supporting the Rubin ramp.

Three new EV models expand the CDMS portfolio: HH unveiled a MODEL A+, a
premium B-segment SUV; MODEL K+, an affordable lifestyle SUV for younger
buyers; and MODEL K, a compact truck targeting Japan’s kei segment and last-mile
logistics. The launches expand its EV reference portfolio to 12, broadening its
contract design and manufacturing service  offering across passenger and
commercial vehicles.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 165: 261103_daiwa_ Fositek

Source PDF: all report pdf/261103_daiwa_ Fositek.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We attended Fositek’s investor meeting following its 3Q26 
results announcement on 3 November. We believe the company will benefit 
from the AI server trend with a rising liquid-cooling penetration rate in the 
following years. With rising server revenue over 2026-28E given its share-
gain potential, we reaffirm our Buy (1) rating. See our previous memo 3Q26 
revenue largely in line with expectations, 7 October 2026.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results beat our estimates and Bloomberg 
consensus. Fositek’s 3Q26 revenue of TWD4,502m (+20.4% QoQ, 
+26.2% YoY), a record high, was in line with our estimate and the 
Bloomberg consensus. Its 3Q26 gross margin came in at 38.0% (vs. 34.0% 
in 2Q26 and 27.7% in 3Q25), also a record-high level, above our estimates 
(34.3%) and the consensus (36.5%), mainly on manufacturing process 
improvements and better-than-expected product mix (server revenue 
accounted for 81.6% in 3Q26, up from 54.9% in 2Q26 and 56.4% in 1Q26). 
Its operating margin of 32.4% (vs. 27.9% in 2Q26 and 22.2% in 3Q25), 
another record high, beat our estimate (28.9%) and the consensus 
(31.2%), on better opex control. Thus, its operating profit of TWD1,460m 
(+39.9% QoQ, +84.2% YoY) beat our estimate and the consensus by 
11.0% and 3.3%, respectively. Its net profit of TWD1,242m (+46.3% QoQ, 
+80.8% YoY) beat our estimate and the consensus by 15.9% and 6.5%, 
respectively. Its 3Q26 EPS finished at TWD18.11. Its 3Q26 product mix 
was: server (81.6%, +92.7% YoY), foldable phone (15.4%, -53.1% YoY), 
notebook (4.5%, -28.1% YoY) and others (0.6%, -10.8% YoY).

Brighter 2027 outlook, driven by server segment. Following the ramping 
up of GPU and ASICs projects in 2H26, we expect a stronger 2027 revenue 
outlook vs. 2026, along with gross margin expansion. With the rise in the 
liquid-cooling penetration rate, quick disconnect (QD) shipments per rack 
and QD spec upgrade for ASP expansion, we forecast its revenue to grow 
by 40.3% YoY in 2026 and 59.2% YoY in 2027. We raise our 2026-28E 
EPS by 9-29% on our more aggressive revenue and gross- and operating-
margin assumptions.

What we recommend: We reaffirm our Buy (1) call and lift our 12M TP to 
TWD2,910 (from TWD2,615), still based on a target PER of 32x (vs. its 
past-3-year range of 9-42x [average of 24x]), translating to a 0.6x PEG 
over 2026-28E on our 1-year forward EPS. Key downside risks: lower-than-
expected market share for key clients; slower-than-expected penetration 
rate of foldable phones; and geopolitical risks.

How we differ: Our 2028E EPS is 8% above the Bloomberg consensus 
likely as we are more aggressive on revenue.

3 November 2026

Company profile 
Fositek is a provider of foldable phone hinges, foldable laptop/tablet hinges, AR/VR glasses hinges, 
ultra-thin laptop hinges and keyboard hinges, as well as hinges for medical instruments and automobile 
applications. Its major clients include Huawei and Motorola.

Fositek Corp (6805 TT): 3 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 7 May 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
The CEO and chairperson of Fositek are not the same individual, and we see no issue with its
board quality. Besides, we note that the chairperson has 24 years of experience in this industry
and took over the role of chairman at Fositek in 2014, which is a positive sign from a governance
perspective.
Capital management 2 Fositek's dividend payout ratio was 40-60% over 2022-25, due likely to its capacity expansionplans.
Related party &
transaction 2 Revenue generated from related-party transactions (mainly for AVC and SZFSD) was 0-16% oftotal revenue during 2022-25, and we found no suspicious activity.
E Supply chainmanagement 1
Fositek maintains smooth communication channels with its suppliers and executes its business
operations effectively. In response to ESG issues, Fositek actively implements supply chain
management, striving to optimise the overall environment through product design, raw-material
procurement, manufacturing, transportation, packaging and recycling processes. It integrates
upstream and downstream suppliers to establish a green supply chain. For example, Fositek has
required its suppliers to sign environmental protection agreements since 2021, committing to
comply with hazardous substance control regulations.
G Data security 2
To protect company information and customer data, Fositek has passed the IEC/ISO 27001
information security management system certification and will continue to enhance execution in
various management areas.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

## Document 208: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서_9ee69886

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

SK하이닉스 (000660)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상  
3Q26 영업이익: 상반기와 다르다. 억제 요인들의 추세 반전 전환 
SK하이닉스의 3Q26 영업이익은 전분기 대비 32% 증가한 80.1조원으로 시장기대
치를 능가할 전망이다. 최근 원화강세 흐름 및 상반기 동사의 상대적 실적개선 속도 
열위에 3Q26 영업이익 추정치는 70조원까지 내려갔다 (컨센서스 74조원). 하지만 
이제 동사 출하량 및 판가의 탄력적 복원세가 경쟁사 보다 빠른 실적 개선을 이끌 전
망이다. 3Q26 DRAM과 NAND 출하량/판가 증감률은 각각 +13%/+22%, 
+4%/+15% 수준으로, 각 영업이익은 63조원/17조원으로 예상된다 (OPM 각각 
84%/69% 추정). 그간의 상대적 실적 열위는 이제 반전된다. 이는 1) 판가 전략에 
있어 중장기적 관점에서 고객 중심 유연성 극대화를 꾀했던 부분의 보상적 되돌림이 
발생하고, 2) 업계에서 제일 높은 HBM 판매 비중으로 인해 판가 상승률에 발목 잡혔
지만, 오히려 HBM4 본격 공급이 시작되며 이익기여가 본격화된다. 아울러 3) 모바
일 핵심고객과의 기성 계약이 산업 전반적 LTA 논의로 재검토되며 공급사에 크게 유
리한 조건으로 변화되어 가기 시작한다. 당사는 1Q27부터 모바일 메모리 판가 급등
을 전망하며 (그림1), 2026E/2027E 영업이익의 경우 264조원/454조원으로 상향조정한
다. 4Q26 내 시장 실적 추정치 상향조정 및 투자자 수급의 구조적 개선을 전망한다.

‘지능기술’ 시대, 수요의 가격 역탄력성 속 중장기 기회창출에 주목 
과거 B2C 사이클과 다르게, 금번 B2B 사이클은 수요의 가격 역탄력성을 보이고 있
다. AGI 도달을 위한 선착순 투자가 핵심이기 때문이다. 빅테크 중 낙오자가 발생하
기 전까지 경쟁적 대규모 투자는 지속된다. 최근 동사의 솔리다임 상장 추진보도에 
일부 우려가 제기되고 있지만, 투자자들은 그 이면의 중장기적 기회에 주목해야 한다. 
동사는 LTA 외에도, 다양한 파트너십을 추진하며 중장기 시장 예측력을 증가시키고 
있다. 솔리다임 상장 과정에서 유입되는 현금은 모회사 ‘SK AI co’의 지분교환 및 JV 
구축 등에 활용되리라 예상된다. 이는 중장기 수요를 ‘창출’시키는 개념이다.

순수한 주주가치 제고 목적 환원 재도출 예상. 적정주가 상향조정 
빠르게 증가한 현금흐름 중 ‘잉여’분은 주주가치 ‘회복’에 우선 배분돼야 함이 마땅하
며, 동사 역시 최근 공시를 통해 주주가치 제고와 주가의 저평가 인식을 드러냈다. 현
재 진행중인 40조원 자사주 매입 프로그램 종료 후 즉각적인 신규 환원 정책 도출이 
예상된다. 2027년 메모리 공급부족은 명확하다. 역사적으로 효율적 시장 내, 주가 조
정은 탄력적 복원으로 귀결되어왔다. 동사에 대한 투자의견 Buy를 유지하며, 적정주
가의 경우 320만원으로 상향한다 (26E/27E 평균 P/B 4.0배 적용). 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 12

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 3,200,000원

상승여력 90.4%

## Document 223: [005380] 현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적저점을 받쳐줄 실적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005380] 현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적저점을 받쳐줄 실적 - 기업.pdf

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 
목표주가 500,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
292,500 
자동차업종

◼ 3Q26 비용절감/환율 효과로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현 
◼ 2027년 물량–환율 영향 상쇄되며 2H27 이익체력 개선 본격화 예상 
◼ 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등에 주목

투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 50만원 하향(-6%) 
현대차 TP 50만원(시총 102조원)은 12MF EPS 41,978원에 Target PER 12배
적용하여 산출. 제한적인 실적 기대 반영해 타겟 밸류 하향함에 따라 TP 조정  
우려 대비 양호한 3Q26 실적으로 주가 하방 부담 완화. 주가의 추세적 상승 
위해서는 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등 확인돼야 할 
것. 기저효과에 따른 물량 개선이 주가 저점 받쳐줄 것이나, 현재 밸류(2027년 
PER 9.1배vs 글로벌 피어 6.1배)에서 추세 상승 이끌긴 어려울 것이기 때문. 
이에, 자동차 본업 외 1) 한/미국 Physical AI 투자 계획 및 2) BD 공급망 구축 
/양산 가시화에 따른 주가 반등 기대

3Q26 매출 45조(YoY -3%, 이하 YoY), OP 2.7조(+6%), OPM 5.9% 시현 
3Q26 OP 추정(2.5조)/컨센(2.4조) 상회. 파업에 따른 생산/판매 차질 영향으로 
기대 제한적이었으나, 비용 절감/환율 영향으로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현. 
[자동차] 매출 33조(-10%), OP 2.1조(+25%), OPM 6.5%. 도매 판매 92.1만대
(-11%). 판매 부진과 원자재 부담에도 불구, 환효과(+1.3조)/비용절감(+0.5조)
로 실적 방어. 이외 YoY 변수별 영향은 물량(-1.1조)/믹스(-0.6조)/원자재(-
0.5조). [금융] 매출 9.2조(+28%), OP 4,710억(-18%), OPM 5.1%, [기타] 매출 
2.9조(+2%), OP 712억(-72%), OPM 2.5% 기록

2027년 매출 191조(YoY +2%, 이하 YoY), OP 12.7조(+15%), OPM 6.6% 전망 
기저효과 바탕으로 연결 도매 물량 YoY +5% 증가 예상하나, 원달러 환율 -
4% 하락(가정 환율 1,380원)하며 1H27 → 2H27로가며 이익체력 개선 예상 
(단위: 십억원, %)

### Page 5

DAISHIN SECURITIES

5  
  현대차 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스콜 QnA 
Q. 금융부문 환율 변동에 따른 4Q26 매출 및 손익 영향 
- 금융부문 환율 영향은 현대차 실적에 미치는 환율 영향과 동일한 방향으로 발생

Q. 3Q26 금융부문 수익성 악화 요인 
- 장기금리 상승에 따른 조달 및 이자비용 증가가 주요인. 자산/부채 만기 매칭에 따른 
금리 상승 영향 반영 
- EV 중심 중고차 가격지수 소폭 하락 및 차량 반환에 따른 매각손실, 금리 상승에 따
른 연체율 및 신용비용 상승 
- 차량 판매가격 인하 영향은 없으며, 3Q26 도매 판매 감소에 따른 금융 영업수익 영향 
불가피했음

Q. 향후 주주환원 정책에서 배당 및 자사주 매입 우선순위 
- 기존 밸류업 정책: 최소 DPS 1만원/TPR 35% 제시. 연간 배당 약 2.6조원 → TPR 
35% 충족 위한 잔여분 자사주 매입/소각 활용 
- 경영환경 변화 고려하여 기존 밸류업 프로그램 전반의 주주가치 제고 방안 재검토중 
- 새로운 정책 확정 및 주주 소통 전까지 기존 약속인 최소 DPS 1만원 유지할 계획 
- 시장의 배당 및 자사주 매입 간 균형 요구를 인지하고 있으며, 주주 의견 수렴 후 주
주가치 제고 계획 공유 예정

Q. 3Q26 영업이익 증감 요인 중 기타 +6,400억 발생 원인 
- 월별 컨틴전시 활동을 통한 비용 최적화 및 재료비 절감 지속 추진 
- 4Q26 원가 절감 효과는 3Q26 대비 확대 예상

Q. 3Q26 영업외손익 감소 원인 
- 3Q26 환율 급락으로 외화 매출채권 등 외화자산 관련 외화환산손실 및 외환거래손실 
발생 
- 매출채권 인식 시점과 회수 시점 간 환율 차이에 따른 외환차손 발생 
- 지분법 대상 기업들의 3Q26 실적 부진에 따른 지분법손익 감소도 일부 영향. 대부분 
외환 관련 손실에서 발생

Q. 중국 OEM 국내 진출 확대에 따른 대응 및 신차/가격 전략 
- 중국 OEM 및 테슬라의 국내 M/S 확대 대응중. 다만, 잦은 가격 조정은 중고차 잔존
가치 및 고객가치 훼손할 수 있어 지양 
- 현대캐피탈의 캡티브 금융 경쟁력 활용. 잔존가치 유예할부 등을 통해 월 납입금을 낮
춰 중국 OEM 대비 경쟁력 확보 
- 서비스 네트워크 기반으로 경쟁사 대비 경쟁력 확보 노력 
- 국내 신차: 4Q26 아반떼 HEV, 투싼, GV90, G80 HEV, 싼타페 FL(2026.12월), G90 
FL 등 출시 예정 
- 북미: 4Q26 투싼, GV80 HEV 출시 및 27년 초 GV90 출시 예상 
- 유럽: 아이오닉3 판매 개시, 4Q26 투싼/베이온 등 주요 모델 출시 예정 
- 중국: 아이오닉 V / 인도: 베이온, 아이오닉 P 등 출시 예정 
- 신차 출시 계획 연도별로 다수 예정. 가격은 원가/경쟁사/소비자 가격 수용성 등을 고
려해 결정

- 일회성 요인보다는 법인별 손익 믹스 및 적용 세율 차이에 따른 영향 
- 4Q26 법인세 수준은 QoQ 큰 변동 없을 것으로 전망

## Document 245: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불리한 환경에서도 성장성 유지삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불리한 환경에서도 성장성 유지불리한 환경에_79fc85e4

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불리한 환경에서도 성장성 유지삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불리한 환경에서도 성장성 유지불리한 환경에_79fc85e4.pdf

 절대 1.9  2.4  (15.2)  
 상대 24.4  20.4  (43.5)  
 절대 (달러환산) 6.1  13.8  (8.6)

3Q26Re: 신규 시설 매출 인식 개시 
연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 6,401억원
(+1.0%, OPM 42.9%)로 컨센서스를 상회하는 실적 기록. 1~4공장이 완전 가동을 유지하는 
한편, 5공장은 PPQ 배치 생산 본격화되면서 매출 기여 확대. 상업 생산 전환이 예상되는 
2H27부터 이익 기여 커질 것으로 기대. 
연초 인수한 Rockville 공장은 3Q에도 안정적인 상업 생산을 이어갔으며 2Q 생산 물량의 
매출이 인식되며 매출 성장에 기여. 동사는 Rockville 공장 매출 인식 등을 반영하여 기존 
+15~20% 성장에서 +20%로 연간 가이던스 상향.   
고객사 수주 회복 기대 
3Q26 기준 누적 수주액은 219억 달러로 전분기 대비 소폭 증가했으나 과거 연간 수주액 
증가 대비 부족한 수준. 저조한 수주 실적은 지난해 초 의약품에 대한 관세 불확실성 증가로 
고객사들의 수주 문의가 저조했던 영향. 26년 초부터 관세 불확실성이 낮아지며 수주 문의
는 회복 추세. 약 1년 정도 논의 기간이 소요되는 것을 고려할 때 26년말~27년 초 수주 회
복 확인 가능할 것으로 기대. 
안정적인 공급망 유지가 중요한 CDMO 산업 특성상 노조 파업 가능성도 고객사 수주를 늦
추는 요인으로 판단. 다만, 10/16 인천 고등법원이 쟁의행위 가처분 항고심에 대해 인용 결
정을 하며 기존 3개 공정에서 9개 공정으로 확대되며 파업 우려는 다소 완화. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원 유지 
매수 의견과 목표 주가 200만원을 유지. 원달러 약세에도 불구하고 5공장 Ramp-up과 
Rockville 공장 매출 기여로 견조한 실적 성장 지속될 것으로 예상. 파업 이슈 해소와 대형 
수주 계약이 주가 상승을 이끌 것으로 판단.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for
every nuance. Lately, there seems to have been more RBA
communication to pore over. The method of delivery has
also evolved. This has sometimes caused confusion when
tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different

tone and varying complexity.
• The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly
using speeches directed to professional audiences to
discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such
as neutral rates and financial conditions. They are also
highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more
ways than just squeezing mortgage holders.
• One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises about the future

path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running
commentary about how it is interpreting recent data. These
interpretations are themselves useful signals about what
the RBA thinks it might do.
Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks
for clues to future rate moves. They will have plenty to pore over
in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry
of fireside chats this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded

interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday. Next
Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before
Senate Estimates, just as the quarterly CPI is released. (This
timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After
the decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a
media release and press conference. More speeches will follow,
including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just

following the RBA Review but also more recently. There may
be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication
outside that certainly feels more frequent than a couple of years
ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little sparse
outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary
appearances. We have counted 58 public engagements so
far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website.
This compares with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in

2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events
were related to economics, markets and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed
as well. There are fewer set-piece speeches on policy and a
Open mouth operations
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few
years ago, there are more interviews across a wide range of

media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It was
a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the
7:30 Report ; it is hard to imagine him agreeing to an Assistant
Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general
audience. Central banks want to explain why they do what
they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising
but the household sector is not the source of the inflation.

Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes
confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief Communication Officer
highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting
media conference is designed to speak to the public. Market
participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
the primary audience. So, if you think that what the Governor
said in the media conference sounded more ‘dovish’ than
what another official says in a more market-oriented forum,

it is partly because she is trying to convey that the RBA
understands that its recent policy actions are making things
harder for many Australians. But this is in much the same way
that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It does, but that
will not stop the treatment.
A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted
material such as the minutes and set-piece speeches than in
unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be

careful with their words in unscripted settings. If the tone
differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have
reduced the pressure to make the set-piece speeches more
accessible. Research on central bank communication often
uses measures such as the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and
Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was

beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison
suggests four speeches from 2026 ( 1, 2, 3, 4) were harder
to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches
scored in the 11 to 12 range, which is high-school-level
“The RBA does not want to promise what it will
do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks. "

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and
15, which is academic or technically dense text requiring
university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-
Governor Phil Lowe also scored better on these metrics than

the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that
readability metrics do not necessarily imply better clarity if the
concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has
evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has begun to put
more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those
invisible ‘star’ variables (NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial
conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences

has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on
the full range of transmission channels from interest rates to
inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour
supply channel). This is understandable in a world of supply
shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt
they would be comforted, though, by the Deputy Governor’s

explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the
smallest of the transmission channels from interest rates
to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on
inflation. It raises the question of whether other policy tools
or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on
inflation through the other channels without hitting a minority
of households disproportionately.

We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases
to summarise the position. From ‘narrow path’ to ‘not
ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once
entrenched, a phrase can outlast its usefulness.
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record
the presentations to the Board and the members’ discussion. The
minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-
meeting statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the

Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The August 2024 minutes
were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how
sensitive the forecasts were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed
Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s
communication have not changed. It wants to explain – and
perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how
it interprets incoming data, especially inflation, the labour

market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ about future moves.
Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular
future path for interest rates. Some academic literature argues
that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high,
for that matter) independently amplifies the transmission
of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-period

interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield
curve. Central banks including the RBA have certainly tried
jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view,
though, is that the potential cost of reputational loss if things
do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a
slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides
the signal about future policy directions. This is why it was

noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour
market as ‘a little tight’ after the September meeting, even
though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We
have been noting for some time that the RBA staff have a
downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the RBA’s
oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy
Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not surprise us.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
While annual retail sales growth improved in September,
rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace of growth
is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very
weak versus history. Consumer spending continues to be

constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to
employment prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five
years into the property downturn, house prices continue to
decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep
downturn, –20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from
annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in high-tech
investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst
domestically oriented businesses as well as property’s

malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and
2028 as the contribution from net exports moderates, broad-
based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect recent
targeted support measures to be broadened and given much
greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr
in September, exceeding both market expectations and the
Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely
driven by energy, fuel prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core

inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services
inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an
immediate concern inflation, the labour market showing
resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to
4.7% in August, suggests demand risks for inflation will need
close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as
the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is released. For both events,

the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The
week after, the US mid-term elections are due.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’
attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor Hauser's fireside
chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a
follow-up rate hike at the November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook
was "materially worse" than the RBA's August forecasts.

Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having
"crystallised" ahead of the September meeting – the Middle
East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser
noted the Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices
are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in August”.
He then positioned AI and data centre developments among
an "extraordinary sequence of positive demand shocks around
the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through

into CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy
Governor noted that "inflation's been coming in at or a little
bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the
labour market". He went on to state that the staff are currently
assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement
on capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other
words, revise up their assumption for the NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of

market pricing for a follow-up November hike, from just a
20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition
today ahead of next week's Q3 CPI. For that release, we are
forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a
0.9 (3.5%yr) increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview
provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3 as
well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast.
Provided inflation prints broadly in line with our expectations,

the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.
Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3
and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to 3.7%yr. Under
the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely
watched measures of core inflation took a step higher to now
be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our
NZ economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise
the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October policy review, while

retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.
Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised  to the upside in
Q3, GDP growth accelerating from 0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting
annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on
track to achieve their 4.5–5.0% full-year growth objective.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
RBNZ now expected to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ will deliver a 25bp hike at its
policy review on 28 October. While September quarter
inflation was slightly below the RBNZ’s forecast, the
underlying detail was firm. More recently, increases in

fuel prices point to a material lift in inflation by year-end.
We expect further interest rate increases over the coming
months, with the OCR reaching a peak of 4% next year.
Softer headline inflation but firm core pressures
leave RBNZ on course to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ to raise the Official Cash Rate
by 25bp at its October policy review (previously, we had
expected a pause at this meeting). This change in view reflects
significant upside risks to near-term inflation relative to the

RBNZ’s previous forecast.
While in line with our forecast, the September quarter inflation
figures were slightly softer than the RBNZ expected, with prices
rising 3.7% over the past year (compared with the RBNZ’s
forecast of 3.9%). However, the details were less reassuring.
Excluding volatile items such as food and fuel, underlying
inflation pressures remained firm. Closely watched measures
of core inflation moved higher and now sit close to, or a little

above, the top of the RBNZ’s target band. We also saw an
increase in the number of goods recording price rises, taking
that measure back to levels seen in the wake of the pandemic.
Alongside the existing firmness in underlying inflation, the
risks for inflation over the coming quarters are firmly tilted to
the upside. That near-term upside risk has also been noted by
the Governor. Following a sharp rise in global fuel costs, we
expect inflation to reach 4.3% by the end of this year (above

the RBNZ’s September MPS forecast for +3.9%). While that is
mainly due to higher prices at the pump, businesses in sectors
such as construction are also reporting related increases in
transport and materials costs. Consistent with those trends,
we expect core inflation to remain in the upper part of the
RBNZ’s target band through 2027.
Reinforcing the case for an OCR hike at the October meeting,
the New Zealand dollar is notably weaker than the RBNZ

assumed: on a trade-weighted basis, the NZD is currently
3.6% below the RBNZ’s September projections. If sustained,
that would add to inflation pressures over the next few
quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted the RBNZ’s
medium-term outlook for inflation.
Several other developments need to be weighed against that
firmer inflation outlook. First, wholesale long-term interest rates
have risen markedly, and there has also been a more moderate

rise in some mortgage rates. The RBNZ is likely to note the role of
those increases in managing medium-term inflation risks.
In addition, the RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that
there is little evidence of a lift in businesses’ longer-term plans
for price setting at this stage in response to the rise in energy
prices (sometimes referred to as “second-round inflation
effects”). We expect the RBNZ to highlight the risk that more
persistent inflation pressures could emerge, depending on the

duration of the oil price shock.
We also expect the RBNZ to note that the labour market
remains cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Employment growth that remains weak relative to labour force
growth, subdued growth in job vacancies and low labour market
turnover are consistent with this view and could be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge that while the
global growth outlook remains solid, there are significant

downside risks from the Iran war and concerns about the
sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Balancing those concerns, we think the MPC will lift the
cash rate next week. However, a vote seems very possible as
differences in view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook has really
shifted in recent weeks.
In September, the bulk of the MPC saw upside risks to the
inflation outlook while two members (Conway and Hansen)

thought the risks were balanced. Recent developments will
not have made those seeing upside risks to inflation more
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
comfortable, while the more dovish MPC members will
likely focus on the ongoing downside risks for growth given
continued softness in house prices and the mounting pressure
on real incomes. Indeed, all MPC members saw some degree
of downside risks to growth which likely remain relevant. All

these messages should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC is likely to continue to present its decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July: a higher
OCR now provides insurance against the need for a much
higher OCR later if upside risks to medium-term inflation
materialise. We doubt the aim will be to push market interest
rates materially higher.
RBNZ to leave door open for further hikes, OCR
still expected to peak at 4% in 2027

In its forward guidance, the RBNZ is likely to leave open the option
of raising the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy
Statement, without presenting an increase as certain. While the
RBNZ will not publish an updated OCR projection at this review,
the press statement is likely to note that further increases may
be needed, depending on economic conditions. The September
projection showed the OCR eventually rising to 3.25%.
We expect the RBNZ will end up delivering another 25bp

hike in December, followed by a further hike in February.
That would take the OCR to 3.5%, roughly where the RBNZ
estimates the neutral rate to be. At that point, we expect the
RBNZ to slow the pace of rate increases (previously, we had
expected that point to be reached a little later).
We continue to expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, taking the OCR to a peak of 4.0%.
While oil prices are generating significant swings in inflation,

underlying pressures remain firm. More restrictive conditions are
likely to be needed to bring core inflation sustainably back to 2%.
Scenarios
Our central scenario, with a probability of around 60%, is that
the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp and signals that a further
increase in December remains an option, without committing to
a markedly tighter future stance than it outlined in September.
Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would see

the OCR increase to 3%, with the RBNZ placing greater
emphasis on upside risks to core and medium-term inflation.
That would suggest an upward revision to the RBNZ’s
projected OCR path in December. Downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the RBNZ leave the OCR unchanged and signal that
the medium-term inflation outlook has not changed
meaningfully from its September forecast, despite higher

near-term inflation. In this scenario, the RBNZ would
reiterate its previous forecast: a 25bp increase in December
and one further hike during 2027.
How might the election play into the decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not be
politically motivated. However, the election outcome is close.
And should it result in a Labour Party-led coalition, it seems
likely the new government would adopt the Labour Party’s

promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual mandate. That would
require the MPC to weigh the degree of labour market slack
more highly in its deliberations. As the degree of labour market
slack currently seems high, a new mandate might justify the
MPC taking more time to return inflation to the middle of the
target range (our earlier note discusses this issue). This could
figure into the deliberations of some MPC members if the
decision between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it

now is viewed as finely balanced.

AUS: Sep Private Sector Credit (%mth)
Oct 30, Last: 0.6, Westpac f/c: 0.5

Private sector credit growth came in slightly firmer than
expected at 0.6%mth in August, above Westpac’s and the
market’s forecast for a 0.5%mth gain, leaving credit growth

running at 8.4%yr. Growth in housing credit continued to ease,

credit persisting.

Oct 28, Last: 2.75, Westpac f/c: 3.00, Mkt f/c: 3.00
We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at
the 28 October Review. Data since September show a higher
outlook for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP starting

point, but increased risks to domestic consumption and
residential investment. On balance, the medium-term inflation

NZ: Oct ANZ Business Confidence (index)
Oct 29, Last: 51.9
Firms remained optimistic in the September survey of business
confidence, with both the activity and inflation measures
broadly holding steady. However, it’s likely that most responses
were submitted in the early part of the month, while fuel prices
took a sharp step higher from the second half of September. The

survey comes too late for the RBNZ’s October policy review, but
evidence about the breadth of cost and price pressures could
have a bearing on the RBNZ’s thinking about the total amount of
policy tightening needed in the months ahead.
NZ: Sep Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Oct 28, Last: 0.2, Westpac f/c: 0.3
The MEI has been gradually ticking higher in recent months,
showing that the jobs market has held its ground through
the Iran conflict and the consequent surge in fuel prices. The

weekly snapshots provided by Stats NZ have been running
ahead of year-ago levels and suggest another solid result for
September – we’ve pencilled in a 0.3% increase. Given the
history of this measure, however, both this month and the
previous month are likely to be revised down a little from their
initial release.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Oct FOMC meeting
Oct 27-28, Last: 3.875%, Mkt f/c: 3.875%, WBC f/c: 3.875%
Core CPI inflation and producer price inflation both surprised
to the upside in September, and the price of Brent oil has
risen back to around USD110. Underlying momentum in
the economy also remains robust, although employment

disappointed in the latest update. Given the concerns shown
by Committee members over price risks and their collective
resolve to return annual inflation to target, the above data
could easily justify a follow-up hike at the October meeting.
That said, Committee members who have spoken recently
have shown patience with respect to assessing the economy
and positioing policy. The market has responded, effectively
pricing out the chance of an October hike in favour of a

December move, which is also our expectation.
US Q3 GDP
Oct 29, %ann’d, Last: 2.2%, Mkt f/c: 3.0%, WBC f/c: 3.1%
The US economy continues to grow at a pace well above trend.
This outperformance is almost entirely due to the strength of
investment in data centres and other AI related assets. The
pipeline for this activity remains strong, limiting near-term
risks to the downside.
Abstracting from AI investment’s contribution, other business
investment and residential investment is weak, weighed down

by high term interest rates and uncertainty related to trade
policy. Household consumption growth is volatile month-to-
month, but the trend is solid. Absent a marked deterioration
in the labour market, which is not our expectation, this
momentum is likely to persist, as wealth continues to grow and
household income gains neutralises inflation. Come 2027 and
2028, growth is expected to slow to trend, but not below.

## Document 324: EcoNews of 2 November 2026 (2026-11-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 2 November 2026 (2026-11-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
5
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.40 / 2 November 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. Data releases confirmed resilient to strong growth and
above-forecast inflation in most advanced economies, with France lag-
ging behind and earning a rating outlook downgrade from Fitch as a
result. Fed, ECB and BoJ remained on hold, as expected, and on track
for December hikes. Oil continues to fluctuate between escalation fears

and hopes for a return to the negotiating table. The tech sell-off in
equity markets intensified despite strong earnings, as markets balk at
large capex plans. This helped bond yields ease from recent highs.
EUROZONE
Resilience confirmed despite tightening credit. GDP expanded by a
stronger-than-expected 0.4% q/q in Q3 (0.2% ex-Ireland), with Spain,
Germany and the Netherlands contributing to the bulk of non-Irish
growth. We increase our growth forecast to 1.6% in 2027 (+0.1pp),

driven by further improving sentiment in manufacturing. Bank lending
conditions tightened further in Q3 for businesses, especially SMEs,
driven by France and Germany, while corporate and housing-loan de -
mand weakened. The ECB kept its deposit rate at 2.5% and signaled it
was firmly on track for a (likely final) December hike. President Lagarde
also confirmed she would step down early, on February 7, setting off
the succession race. EU leaders are expected to decide jointly on the

three Board members departing between January and May (President,
chief economist, and market operations), likely at their mid-December
meeting. Official candidates to succeed Lagarde are BIS Head Pablo
Hernandez de Cos and former Dutch central bank Governor Klaas Knot.
To watch next week: October flash CPI (Wednesday).
France: Another growth setback, with silver linings
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 as a sharp drop in aeronauti -
cal production hit exports and investment. However, services out -

put grew by a robust 0.3% q/q, and household consumption also
grew more strongly than forecasted (+0.4%). While 2026 will ine -
vitably be knocked down (to 0.3%), we forecast 0.8% for 2027.
Nevertheless, Fitch issued an unscheduled rating outlook downgrade
to negative territory, on grounds that fiscal, growth and ad -
justment prospects have deteriorated since its August review.
A new review could be concluded as early as February. Harmonised
inflation accelerated to 3.8% y/y in October, with both energy and

non-energy contributing. Meanwhile, producer price inflation accele -
rated to 8.2% y/y in September. Insee’s index of household confidence
fell to 80, matching its 2013 all-time low, driven by a decline in ex -
pectations of its own financial situation. Private payroll employment
declined by 26,000 over Q3 (-0.1%). However, in previous quarters, the
decline in payroll employment was more than offset by the increase in
self-employment (not released until November 27).

To watch next week: September industrial production (Thursday); Sep-
tember foreign trade (Friday).
Germany: Growth holds up as inflation slows
GDP grew by 0.2% q/q in Q3, with consumption rising slightly but in -
vestment declining and export growth slowing. HICP inflation fell from
3.3% to 2.8% in October following energy-tax cuts and fuel discounts,
but core inflation also slowed, to 2.1%. Business confidence (Ifo survey)
reached its highest level in more than three years, led by manufactu -

ring, which is back in positive territory for the first time since 2023.
By contrast, consumer confidence deteriorated to its lowest since May,
due to growing concerns about inflation, which are weighing on consu-
mers’ spending intentions.
To watch next week: September retail sales (Monday); September fac-
tory orders (Thursday); September industrial production (Friday).
Italy: Stagnant growth meets energy-driven inflation surge
GDP was flat in Q3, missing expectations, as positive domestic demand

was offset by net exports. Economic sentiment improved, but HICP in -
flation jumped from 4.2% to 5.6% in October, its highest level since
September 2023, mainly because of regulated energy prices; core in -
flation also rose, to a more moderate 2%. More positively, economic
sentiment rose to 100.2 in October 2026 (from 99.4 in September),
despite the Eurozone decline. The improvement was driven by services
and construction. The Italian cabinet approved a budget focused on

middle-class tax cuts, financed partly by levies on banks, energy and
gambling companies. x
UNITED KINGDOM
A cautious budget buys breathing space. The budget offered 98 small
cost-of-living alleviation measures and £4.1 billion in household ener-
gy cost relief, while only modestly increasing taxes, including a tem -
porary rise in the bank surcharge. Its short-term stance is slightly ex -
pansionary, while major structural decisions on defence, pensions and

welfare have been postponed. The official budget watchdog, OBR, cut
its 2027 growth forecast to 1%, and firms’ inflation expectations conti-
nued to rise. UK gilts outperformed and equities advanced after the
budget. GBP rose to a six-week high against the dollar and a 19-month
high versus the euro as markets price a near-certain Bank of England
rate hike (to 4%) at this week meeting. We agree but, unlike market
pricing, we do not expect it to hike further.

To watch next week: Bank of England interest-rate decision (Thursday).
UNITED STATES
Fed awaiting more information despite strong growth. The Federal Re-
serve held its target range at 3.75–4.0%, with two members voting for a
25 bp increase, as Chair Warsh highlighted stronger growth, favourable
employment conditions and inflation that remains too high. Q3 GDP
accelerated to 3.4% annualised, powered by resilient consumption and
a 12.7% jump in business investment. Despite jobless claims remaining

near multi-decade lows, compensation costs adjusted for inflation de-
clined 0.4%, marking the second consecutive quarter in which labour
cost growth trailed price increases.
To watch next week: Midterm elections (Tuesday); October ISM ma -
nufacturing (Monday) and non-manufacturing (Wednesday); Q3 non-
farm productivity (Thursday); October non-farm payrolls (Friday).
JAPAN
BoJ on hold with underlying inflation “consistent with the 2% tar -
get”. The Bank of Japan kept its policy rate at 1.25%, with one dissent

in favour of a hike. Its outlook judged underlying inflation to be
consistent with the 2% target, a major shift in its assessment, although
Governor Ueda mentioned risks on both sides in his remarks, stres -
sing in particular the risk of inflation overshooting the target. It signals
continued gradual monetary adjustment, with a next hike expected in
December. Alongside the rate hold, the BOJ announced it would near -
ly triple the pace of ETF sales starting in January, to approximately

JPY 860 billion per year. Japanese equities shrugged off the announ -
cement.
To watch next week: Minutes of the September Bank of Japan meeting
(Thursday); September wage growth (Friday).

### Page 2

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.40 / 2 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
EMERGING ECONOMIES
ASIA
China: Monetary policy reform and no growth signals
The PBOC disclosed a 1.25% overnight reverse-repo rate and an -
nounced that the overnight rate would become its principal policy
lever, starting 2nd November. It also pledged to develop cross-border
renminbi payments and introduced a liquidity backstop for non-bank

financial institutions. The 5th Plenum of the 20 th Central Committee of
the CCP focused on party discipline and crackdown on corrupt cadres,
without broader policy signals. Chinese equities had their worst month
since 2016, falling 8.5% in October, as doubts over AI earnings deepe -
ned investor pessimism. The PMIs published on 30 th October suggest
economic activity is losing momentum; in the manufacturing sector,
the RatingDog PMI fell slightly to 52 (from 52.1) while the official PMI

fell more sharply and dropped below 50 (49.3 vs. 50.1 in the previous
month).
South Korea and Taiwan: AI boom lifts growth even higher
In Korea, GDP grew by 1.0% q/q and 3.4% y/y in Q3, supported by boo -
ming semiconductor exports linked to global AI demand. The expansion
was uneven, however, as private-consumption growth slowed marke -
dly. In Taiwan, GDP surged by 3.2% q/q and 14.4% y/y in Q3, beating
expectations and accelerating from the previous quarter as technology

firms expanded capacity to meet strong demand.
EMEA
Poland: Lower inflation gives monetary policy some room
Headline inflation declined from 4.0% to 3.5% in October as electricity,
gas and transport fuel prices rose more slowly, helped by a temporary
fuel-price cap. Rates are likely to remain unchanged in the near term,
although renewed zloty weakness means a hike cannot be ruled out.
Saudi Arabia: Reserves fall as regional pressures intensify
Official reserve assets declined by 4.4% m/m in September to

SAR 1.75 tn, their lowest level since December, with foreign-currency
assets recording particularly sharp monthly and annual declines.
Türkiye: Pressure on lira and FX reserves
The lira headed for its largest monthly fall in 15 months and cen -
tral-bank reserves came under renewed pressure. This is the conse -
quence of the warning by MSCI that Türkiye could lose its emer -
ging-market status if measures to improve transparency and market
supervision following the Fund scandal are deemed insufficient.

LATIN AMERICA
Brazil’s post-election rout: stocks and real plunge, yields surge. Bra -
zilian markets, expecting a Flavio Bolsonaro victory, sold off sharply
after Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva won a fourth presidential term. The Ibo-
vespa fell 14% Monday — its largest daily drop since COVID-19 — and
ended the week down 18%. The real fell over 11% intraday and ended
the week down 8% against the euro. Meanwhile the yield on Brazil’s
10-year government bonds rose 250 bp to 15.5%, reaching its highest

level in over a decade. The selloff reflects fiscal concerns over pledges
to raise the minimum wage and Bolsa familia benefits and to offer free
public transport on weekends and bank holidays. Finance Minister Da-
rio Durigan’s pledge to seek a higher 2027 primary surplus, without a
new target, did little to reassure markets.
COMMODITIES
The oil market continues to fluctuate between escalation fears and
hopes for a return to the negotiating table. Brent prices were down by

5% over the week, reaching USD 109/b on Monday. Tensions on physical
supply continue to decline. Ditto for diesel crack spreads. On the back
of geopolitical tensions, lower-than-requested EU stock level and spe-
culation over a cold snap in Europe in November, European gas prices
(TTF) recorded another strong weekly rise and have breached the
EUR 100/MWh for the first time since Dec 2022). The Asian gas bench-
mark (JKM) is following the same trend but at a slower pace (4.6% w/w

to USD 32 /MBtu, equivalent to around EUR 96.3 /MWh).
TO WATCH NEXT WEEK
Global: Manufacturing PMIs for EM and final DM prints (Monday); final
services and composite PMIs (Wednesday).
y/y = year-on-year
m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point
mb = million barrels
MBtu = Million British Thermal Units

## Document 327: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ November 2-6

Source PDF: all report pdf/ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ November 2-6.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-31
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: November 2-6
Date:    3 hours ago
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: November 2-6

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
November 2-6
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 31 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

The S&P 500 fell 0.6% last week, and the Nasdaq dropped 2.0%, both
posting their third straight week of losses, as disappointing tech earnings
revived concerns about AI-related spending.
The FOMC held the federal funds rate (FFR) at 3.75%-4.00% on
Wednesday in a 10-2 vote, with the dissenters favoring a 25bps hike. We
believe the committee needs another 50bps of increases to get back to a
neutral FFR that is neither accommodative nor restrictive.

October’s employment report (Fri) caps a jam-packed week of labor
market data, including JOLTS (Tue), ADP (Wed), and Challenger and
jobless claims (Thu). ISM’s M-PMI (Mon) and NM-PMI (Wed) are also
due. Six Fed officials are scheduled to speak. Tuesday’s midterm
elections should be interesting. Rate decisions from the Reserve Bank of
Australia and the Bank of England and earnings reports from Apple,
Palantir, AMD, and Qualcomm round out the week's calendar. Here’s

more:
(1) Employment. October’s employment report is the economic headliner.
Payrolls rose 29,000 in September, lowering the three-month average to
50,700 (chart). The unemployment rate rose to 4.2% from 4.1%, while
average hourly earnings increased just 0.1% m/m. We expect a stronger
payroll gain than the consensus, of approximately 55,000.
Challenger’s October layoff announcements (Thu) follow September’s
count of 43,300. Technology led September’s announced cuts, at 10,800.

Initial unemployment claims (Thu) remain low, with a four-week average of

### Page 4

197,800 for the week ended October 24 (chart). We expect more of the
same for both labor market indicators this week.
The four-week moving average of ADP's employment measure peaked at
26,000 in late September before easing to 16,750 in early October (chart).
September’s monthly ADP report showed a 90,000 gain in private
payrolls, nearly double the Bureau of Labor Statistics’ 46,000.
September’s JOLTS report (Tue) follows August’s 7.1 million job openings,

down from 7.3 million in July. The share of consumers saying jobs are
“plentiful” was 26.7% in October, little changed from 27.0% in August
(chart).

### Page 5

(2) PMIs. ISM’s M-PMI (Mon) follows September’s 54.5 reading, with the
prices-paid index at 77.9. The NM-PMI (Wed) was 54.9 (chart). S&P
Global’s flash M-PMI edged up to 56.0 in October from September’s 55.9,
a 53-month high, while the flash NM-PMI eased to 57.9 from 58.8. Both
still point to solid ISM readings.
S&P 500 forward earnings rose 38.8% y/y as of October 26, up from
37.2% in September, suggesting more upside for the M-PMI (chart).

### Page 6

(3) Earnings. The earnings reporting season continues this week, with
31% of the S&P 500 companies set to report. Apple, Palantir, AMD, Arista
Networks, Qualcomm, and Arm headline the list.
Analysts’ consensus estimates imply Q3-2026 operating EPS growth for
S&P 500 companies of 23.8% y/y, down slightly from 24.1% last week,
after Alphabet, Amazon, and Intel missed estimates (chart). Q4 growth
estimates held at 30.2%.
(4) Global. The Reserve Bank of Australia (Tue) is expected to hike its

cash rate target by 25bps for a second straight meeting, to 4.85% from
4.60% (chart). The markets are pricing in 88% odds of that result, so it’s
no sure thing. Last week, the Reserve Bank of New Zealand held its
official cash rate at 2.75% despite 95% odds of a hike. Both central banks
are often seen as bellwethers for the global rate cycle.

### Page 7

The Bank of England (Thu) looks set to raise its rate by 25bps to 4.00%,
with 98% odds priced into the markets. The markets see a 35% chance of
another hike in December.
The Eurozone’s flash CPI (Wed) is expected to rise in October from
September’s 3.8% y/y, with the core rate holding at 2.5%. Among the
major economies, Spain’s headline rate was the highest at 5.0% in
September and Germany’s the lowest at 3.3% (chart).

## Document 345: France’s manufacturing rebound offers some relief, but no turning point yet

Source PDF: all report pdf/France’s manufacturing rebound offers some relief, but no turning point yet.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

French manufacturing output rose for a second consecutive
month in September, led by a sharp rebound in car production.
But with output still below last year’s level and construction
ﬁrmly in recession, industry can only soften, not reverse, the
economy’s stagnation
Charlotte de Montpellier
French manufacturing output rose for a second consecutive month in
September
Manufacturing output returns to its June
level
There is ﬁnally some better news from the French economy.

Manufacturing output increased by 0.5% in September, following a
0.3% rise in August, bringing production back to its June level. The
improvement was relatively broad-based. Food production
rebounded by 1.0%, while output of electrical, electronic and
